ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-16-sejm-05-2.u138	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! [[Game in the O.R., bell ringing.]] It is with great pleasure that I present to the Sejm a draft law introducing in our country a long-awaited tax on certain financial institutions, commonly called banking tax. In this way, one of the main points of the Law and Justice programme is being implemented, through which the voters entrusted their governments to our group. It is good and fair that financial institutions will finally have to participate more than ever in the obligation to pay for the necessary budget expenditure. Unfortunately, the scale of propaganda jazz and manipulation against the introduction of this tax still shows the strength of the lobby of financial institutions in our country, the strength of the wealthiest lobby. It is the bank tax that bears witness that the Law and Justice are the political environment that is courageously in the interest of Poles against strong, powerful pressure groups, especially international but also national. Let's start with basic information. It is obvious that the mystery has shown that financial institutions, mostly linked to international capital, have enjoyed and so far enjoyed high privileges in relation to all the rest of their business. Banking activity in our country brings exceptional revenues. The profitability of these institutions is much higher than the profitability of similar entities in the West, while bank charges and commissions on loans in Poland have been much higher for years than abroad. Some time ago, the public was appalled by the fact that we are the fourth largest country in Europe, in which participation in the income of financial institutions of this type, those revenues which constitute fees and commissions from customers, is up to 27%. Therefore, bank charges and commissions were much higher than those which Polish citizens could bear in relation to their earnings. In fact, there is no healthy competition in this sector. If that were the case, we wouldn't have had to pay so much for loans and so little for deposits deposited with banks. It is fortunate that during the many-year reigns of previous teams they failed to sell the remains of state elements in this system. Thanks to this, the Minister of Finance can say today that the threat of increased fees by banks as a result of this bill cannot be realised even because PKO Bank Polski will not raise such charges. You can see from here that it is necessary for you to have strategic property in such a sector. The banking system in today's countries all over the world has a huge basic privilege - the decision to grant a loan or loan actually allows businesses or businesses and citizens to buy, and banks charge much higher interest than the costs incurred by these banks. The profits of the banking sector amount to several billion zlotys a year. Many of the revenue generated is carried out abroad to capital-related maternal banks. Polish entrepreneurs, especially small and medium-sized ones, often cannot rely on fair access to credit decisions. It is also worth mentioning the additional charges caused by the specific compulsion to hold a bank account charged with additional charges, the charges associated with the use of ATM and credit cards. And the banking and insurance system has been giving us real propaganda raids in the press, radio, television and Internet for weeks, which notabene are very heavily funded by advertising through this system passed on. The amounts of advertising in the media are amounts of hundreds of millions of zlotys per year. You can see how proper this project is, how it is at odds with the interests of an environment that has so far been convinced of its infallibility and impunity. We are talking about a sector whose assets are worth PLN 1880 billion. These are banks and other institutions of this type. In addition, insurers with an amount of 180 billion. Together it is over 2 trillion PLN. Poland was a country where the poor paid for the rich until the rule of law and justice. The lowest pensions, pensions and lowest wages are taxed, while VAT is paid primarily by consumers, those with low and medium income, and the wealthiest often avoid paying taxes. VAT revenues are falling, corporate tax revenues are falling in relation to economic development, and multinational corporations find clever tricks to artificially increase costs and thus show a loss or income so small that income tax is hardly paid. The result is that over the years we have heard that it is impossible to introduce decent and fair supplements to raise children, for example, that tax burdens cannot be removed from the poorest. This project deals with a kind of pressure on financial institutions to contribute PLN 6 to 7 billion annually to the state budget. The introduction of this tax is supported by millions of citizens, and from the opinion polls published a few days ago by the Newspaper of Legal Affairs, it appears that as many as 41% of people in our country support this tax, only 19% are against Despite what I call propaganda raids on the part of those who oppose the introduction of this tax. We will be one of the last countries in Europe to introduce such a tax, and hence, I think, this very fierce, even angry resistance, talking about some kind of racket, about the fact that some wasteful budget expenditure is to be financed from it. And what is the purpose of this tax? The purpose of this tax is simply to obtain an additional source of financing for budgetary expenditure, in particular social expenditure, as mentioned in the government programme by Prime Minister Beata Szydło. On this occasion I will remind you of the story, when in 2012 the increase in VAT was introduced, supposedly temporary, for a very short period, by Mr Jan Vincent-Rostowski's finance minister. [[Voice from the room: Take her down.]] Then Law and Justice offered... [[Voice from the room: Take her down and it's done.]] ...that instead of this tax, instead of increased VAT, which will be charged primarily to the poorest in our country, or next to it, to introduce a bank tax in parallel with it. This proposal was then rejected. This Act aims to increase the participation of the financial sector in the financing of budgetary expenditure. The increase in VAT rates by one percentage point in recent years increased the tax burden on the public by an amount similar to the estimated revenue of the State budget It's because of the tax I've been paying. It is justified for the financial sector to be more involved in tax burden-bearing than has been the case in other European countries. Such a tax, which operates in several countries of the European Union, was introduced subsequently after 2008. The most common subject matter of taxation is: the value of the balance sheet total, with different reservations, the net asset value, the value of the liabilities and the value of the total capital requirement. Such taxes have been in operation in the UK since 2011, in Hungary since 2010, in Portugal since 1 January 2011, in France since 1 January 2011, in Sweden since October 2008. According to the Act, the tax will include the sum of the value of the taxpayer's assets resulting from the statement of turnover and balances drawn up by the taxpayer on the basis of the accounts of the principal account at the end of each month or in accordance with the standards applied by taxpayers under the Accounting Act of more than PLN 4 billion. In the case of national banks, branches of foreign banks and branches of credit institutions and cooperative savings and credit banks, the Act provides for a reduction in the basis for the value of own funds and those amounts which the taxpayer, acting in implementation of the decision, has increased over the period of one month for which the taxable amount is determined. The tax will be settled monthly and the tax rate for each month will be 0.0325% tax bases for domestic banks, branches of foreign banks, branches of credit institutions and cooperative savings and credit banks and 0.05% tax bases for national insurance undertakings, national reinsurance undertakings, branches of foreign insurance undertakings and foreign reinsurance undertakings and major branches of foreign insurance undertakings and foreign reinsurance undertakings. The law was constructed on the basis of the construction elements of the tax, i.e. sets out, in turn, the subject matter, the entity, the tax obligation, the tax base, the tax rate, the way in which the tax is collected and the exemption and tax relief. The subject of taxation is the sum of the value of the taxpayer's assets resulting from the balance sheet, and therefore it is a property tax, with the taxable amount being reduced by the value of the own funds held by taxpayers engaged in credit-loan activities due to the need to protect their interests as depositaries. In view of the situation of the taxpayer in respect of which the Financial Supervision Authority has been forced to exercise its statutory powers, no tax will be levied during the period during which the legal effects of decisions issued by KNF, which are, for example, the subject of the liquidation of the bank, the suspension of the bank's activities and the request for its bankruptcy or the establishment of a bank's management or the compulsory liquidation of an insurance undertaking, a reinsurance undertaking, the establishment of a commercial management of such an insurance and reinsurance undertaking or the application for the bankruptcy of an insurance and reinsurance undertaking, and the establishment and cancellation of a curator or the establishment of a commercial manager in a cooperative credit and savings bank. The subject of taxation are legal persons, i.e. banks, insurance and reinsurance undertakings and cooperative credit and savings banks, and organisational units without legal personality, i.e. branches of foreign institutions. In addition to taxation, foreign insurance companies from the Member States of the European Union operating in Poland on the basis of freedom of insurance activities and banks carrying out cross-border banking activities remain. Taxing these entities would be extremely difficult, and their current scale of activities in Poland outside branches is not significant. Tax collection will follow the principle of tax self-calculation by taxpayers. The tax will be charged on a monthly basis. Such a solution is supported by the drawing up, during such periods, of sets of turnovers and balances giving rise to the possibility of establishing a tax base, but also by the reporting obligations already in place in the legal system of those institutions carried out in monthly cycles. As the competent tax authority in the Act, the head of the tax office competent locally because of the residence of the taxable person has been appointed, except that in the case of branches of financial institutions, the head of the tax office competent locally because of the head office of the branch. The Act provides for a tax exemption of the balance sheet total, as I mentioned, of a value not exceeding PLN 4 billion. The exemption is introduced in order to protect emerging banks and insurance undertakings which have a positive impact on competition on the market, since their tax treatment could lead to financial difficulties. Similar solutions exist in other countries, for example in Austria up to EUR 1 billion is released. It was also decided to exempt from the tax Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego and possibly other state banks which may arise in the future. Such institutions shall pursue the economic policy objectives of the State and shall be set up for purposes other than strictly commercial. Their taxation, given that the assets are equipped by the Polish State, makes no sense and would generate only an empty circulation of money instead of pursuing the fiscal objectives set out in the Act. The Act also contains transitional provisions allowing for the collection of tax in the first period of the tax settlement, i.e. in February 2016 Moreover, for fiscal reasons, it is proposed to amend the Corporate Income Tax Act, which aims to prevent deduction of the proposed corporate income tax base. The discussion on the introduction of this legal solution highlights very strongly the fact that the introduction of this tax would have an impact on the level of fees and commissions applied by banks. In literature, this problem is also considered and examined in terms of tax already introduced in other countries, both within the European Union and outside the European Union. Many professionals, including those who do not necessarily support the introduction of such a tax, point out that the impact on the level of fees for customers is either small or negligible or none. Therefore, there is a great deal of evidence, also in the professional, scientific, expert work, which is precisely related to the examination of this impact. The tax, because of its shape, does not interfere with the level of minimum reserves as this level remains in the exclusive competence and competence of the central bank. The importance of this tax will only be disclosed when assessing the capital adequacy. Capital adequacy, i.e. the solvency ratio, is the minimum percentage ratio of net own funds to risk-weighted assets. Its minimum value is 8% and at the end of the first quarter of 2015 it was 13.6% in our country for the whole sector. Bank law in Article. 128 calls own funds net of internal capital and imposes an obligation on banks and the right to determine their own amount in relation to the risk profile generated by the bank. The KNF keeps track of the accuracy of the bank's allocation of this capital. Internal capital or own funds are funds designed to ensure that the bank continues to operate in the event of materialisation of all the risks estimated for the asset structure. In simplification, internal capital is part of the share capital and backup capital created from retained earnings. The increase in demand for this capital may be due to the intention to expand credit or increase the level of risk generated by the current structure of influences... In nominal terms, the sector's own funds are PLN 125 billion for commercial banks and PLN 10,5 for cooperative banks. In December, I apologize, on 30 September 2015, the total amount of assets of the sector as a whole, as I have already mentioned, amounted to PLN 1887 billion, i.e. tax at the rate 0.0325% monthly is about 7.3 billion per year. The net financial result of the sector amounted to 16.2 billion, i.e. it increased by 7.1%. in relation to 2013. With almost zero inflation and relatively small economic growth, this is an excellent result. According to KNF, the balance sheet total of the insurance sector as at 30 June 2015 was 180 billion and the net financial result after 6 months is almost PLN 3.5 billion. These figures show a higher profitability of the insurance sector vis-à-vis the banking sector, which justifies a higher tax rate of 0.05% monthly. In conclusion, it must be stated that the proposed law is a law so designed that it will enable the state budget to carry out those tasks which were announced and for which the Poles voted in the recent elections. These tasks, which relate in particular to supporting family policy, in particular to reducing the burden of the least earner. On the other hand, in the banking sector, it is designed not to bring financial problems in this sector, but to ensure that, in proportion to its share of economic growth, it also participates in financing development projects, changes in the social policy of the state, which will in future contribute to the better economic situation of the country as a whole. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]] [[President of the session includes Deputy Marshal Barbara Dolniak Sejm]]

ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-16-sejm-05-2.u204	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of Members of the Polish People's Party a bill on a one-off allowance for certain pensioners, pensioners and persons receiving pre-retirement benefits, pre-retirement benefits, bridge pensions or teacher compensation benefits, print project No 80. This project meets the expectations of 6.5 million beneficiaries. I will provide a justification for this project. In accordance with the provisions of the Law of 17 December 1998 on pensions from the Social Insurance Fund, annual valorization is carried out from 1 March using a percentage rate of valorization, the main basis of which is the average annual price index of consumer goods and services in the previous calendar year. The average annual price index of goods and services may be calculated against households of pensioners and pensioners, or in total, for all households, whichever is more favourable. The valorisation rate is therefore the higher indicator for consumer goods and services prices, which is increased by at least 20% the actual increase in the average salary in the previous calendar year. So here we don't have an upper limit... i.e. 20% minimum , not more than 50%. It follows from the above that all benefits are paid in an amount increased by a percentage of the same for the lowest and highest benefits, which in turn means that those who receive the lowest benefits receive significantly less than those who receive the higher benefits. This problem has raised serious discussions among the recipients for many years. We believe that it is right, because there is a separation between the lowest and very high or very high benefits. This is socially unfair, so there have been various attempts here to change this valorization. It's pretty controversial. The Polish People's Party even proposed a transition to quota valorization at a certain time, which would be fairer. A little history. In 2009, the valorisation rate was 106.1%. which gave 85 zlotys of valorization. In 2010, the valorisation rate was 104%. That was 70 zlotys. The valorization rate... In 2011 it was only 103 and only 50 PLN. That's what raised pensions have been. In order to protect the household budget of the lowest recipients in 2012, all pensioners and pensioners received equal increases in benefits of PLN 71. In 2013, the valorisation rate was 104, the average increase was 70 PLN, about 70 PLN, while in 2014 pension benefits were increased by 101%. and the average increase was PLN 29.87. In 2015, pension benefits were an increased valorisation rate of 100,68%, which was very low. However, the principle has been introduced that valorisation must not be lower than 36 PLN. It was also the way out towards these lowest valorizations, the lowest benefits. The predicted valorization rate, the esteemed High Izbo, for 2016 is 100.52%. Both the forecast of the average annual price index of goods and services in 2015 and the forecast of the average annual price index of consumer goods and services for households is very low due to the deflation this year. This is negative - 99,8%. Let me remind you that we have a deflation of 0.2% this year. On the other hand, the projected increase in average wages in the national economy in 2015 is 3.6%, which means that the projected 20% the real increase in wages for 2015 is 0.72%. As a result of the above forecasts and the increase in prices of goods and services such a calculated valorisation next year would amount to PLN 4.57 gross. This could, of course, exceed the cost of the operation itself in many situations, because the conversion itself, sending the documents to the recipients is also a big cost. Therefore, for 2016, in addition to the valorisation of pension benefits by the projected rate - 0,52% , a group of Members, Members of the Polish People's Party, coming out, as I said at the beginning, in response to the expectations and difficult situation of those recipients who are in the most difficult situation, receive the lowest benefits, propose to pay one-off allowances for benefits that do not exceed 2 000. zł. This is the limit amount to which this allowance would benefit next year. The amount of one-off allowance for recipients with the lowest benefits, i.e. up to 900 PLN, will be 350 PLN. And here there is gradual degression: over 900 zł to 1100 zł it will be 300 zł, and over 1500 zł to 2000 zł it will be 100 zł. In our solutions, we propose that the allowances be paid out of office, not on request, but on behalf of the public, in the performance of March, which means that it would not be any additional operation, that is, at the same time every pensioner in March would receive this additional payment. The decision to grant such benefit will be issued and the allowance paid by the competent pension authority. In the event of a coincidence of such benefits, only one benefit shall be paid. The rulers have a duty, and the rulers have the right to demand that their benefits developed during a long period of contribution are not lost in their real meaning. Therefore, we value both pensions and pensions every year, based on two indicators: average increases in consumer prices and services and wage increases. The year 2015, which is the basis for calculating the amount of these benefits in the following year, 2016 brought 0,2% deflation, so the zloty was strengthened, and small - here it is a pity, but such - 0.72% , salary increases. In many discussions on the valorisation of benefits of this kind, there are voices that the percentage valorisation is not fair, because smaller pensions are, for obvious reasons, revalorised at a lower percentage, because they come from smaller amounts, larger ones - in greater but the decisions and case law of the Constitutional Tribunal in this matter are clear. Allow me to quote: Pension benefits are paid in exchange for the insurance contribution paid into the scheme, but the sum of the contributions paid does not ultimately determine the amount of the pension. The formula for calculating benefits expresses the basic principles of the insurance system, in particular the principle of social solidarity. This means that the benefits paid must remain in proportion to the income the insured person had before retirement, but must also satisfy the basic needs of the insured person. Let me remind you that this is a mechanism that assumes annual price-paid valorisation, regardless of price index and wage growth. Earlier, let me remind you, we had a valorisation mechanism that only assumed the benefit of pensions when the price increase in consumer goods and services exceeded 5%. And we remember that at that time the values were not annual - in the extreme case, if I remember correctly, we waited three years for valorization. So I'm sure what we have right now is a better solution from this point of view. In short - I am not an economist - that this mechanism that we have now, the mechanism of price-wage valorisation, assumes the possibility of the participation of pensioners in the effects of economic growth, because what is characteristic of economic growth is the rise in wages. So if we have an element of wage valorization in this mechanism, he assumes, however, that when this economic growth takes place, it will occur... [[Yes.]] ...it is through this element that the increase in pension pay will grow faster than in the case of the increase resulting from the preservation of their existing purchasing power. ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-16-sejm-05-2.u346	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! The process of closing the programmes from the 2007 to 2013 perspective is a multi-stage process. And, in principle, the first moment at which the measures will be completed from the 2007 to 2013 perspective is on 31 December, which is the date of the so-called. the final date of eligibility. Then all expenditure which will be reimbursed by the European Union in the following years must be borne. Until 30 June 2016 we have time to submit the so-called. intermediate payments and next year, next year, 31 March 2017 is the deadline for the submission of closure documents and the request for final payment. With regard to the Member's question on the possibility of losing funds in the area of railways or infrastructure investments, today we are talking rather about the use of mechanisms that will allow us to use funds in these areas, and that is, among other things. rail, where they cannot be used, transfer to areas where there has been overcontracting, the use of mechanisms that will enable the full absorption of EU funds by Poland. From this perspective, one of the first priorities of the Minister for Development was the preparation of a recovery plan, which was adopted by the Council of Ministers on 7 December. One of its elements is to increase the amount of over-contracting, i.e. the use of funds that can be allocated to projects, and to search for projects that could potentially be financed under EU programmes. Applications for over-contracting from both national and regional programmes are currently being examined. The second mechanism, which is a mechanism relatively easy to adapt, is the mechanism of so-called flexibility, i.e. the use of up to 10% appropriations under the priorities in which we have a financial surplus, an account of these areas, those priorities in each operational programme in which we have no use. Efforts have also been made to adopt changes in the 2007-2013 perspective programmes. At the moment, changes in three national programmes are being examined: in the Operational Programme - Infrastructure and environment - Operational Programme - Innovation economy - Eastern Poland - under the European Regional Development Fund - also under the European Social Fund, the Operational Programme - Human capital, concerning the so-called. a revision of the certification base, which will actually allow for the addition of funds and the creation of more favourable clearing mechanisms with the European Commission. This option was also indicated in regional programmes, i.e. regional programmes. They can take advantage of that, too. So that speaking here about railway projects, or about the mentioned Myscowa from the perspective of December 2015, it is difficult to talk about the possibility of financing these projects. This is a long-term process. In fact, at this point in time, this is rather an attempt to find opportunities that allow us to respond flexibly to the situation that is in the individual programmes. Now on the scale of the challenges. On Monday, 14 December remained until settlement approx. 22 billion euro and these funds should be settled first and foremost by June next year. The government is making every effort and the Minister for Development to ensure that Poland does not lose the euro from the 20072013 perspective. And here, certainly, all the efforts that can be made are made at both national and European level. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

However, I think it is worth going back to the way in which the programme was adopted, because this is very significant, very interesting. Let me give the High House some information on this. Work on the new national road construction programme from the perspective of 2014 and 2020 started already in 2013 with the adoption of Annexes 5 and 6 to the national road construction programme for 2011 and 2015 because there were such prosthetics of the programme adopted for a shorter period than it should have been, but let us leave this. The introduction of the above-mentioned annexes was the first step in developing a comprehensive road construction programme for the new financial perspective of the European Union, the perspective of 2014.202022. All tasks included in the existing Annexes 5 and 6 due to ongoing tendering or signed contracts have been automatically integrated into the 2014-2023 national road construction programme (with a view to 2025). On 4 June 2013, the previous government adopted a resolution introducing Annex 5 to the programme, i.e. a list of strings implemented in the new financial perspective. Annex 5 has been amended several times. At the time of joining the work on the new road programme, Annex 5 was in force adopted by Resolution No 158/2014 of the Council of Ministers with a financial limit of PLN 67 billion. On 1 October 2013, the previous government adopted a resolution introducing Annex 6, which additionally increased the amount of tasks by PLN 5 billion. The first assumptions of the national road construction programme set the financial limit for new road investments at PLN 92 billion, so it was known that at that time it had a huge chance to implement it. The above-mentioned investment titles were to be included in the then Annex 1 to the programme, the basic list with the amount of PLN 92.8 billion. So it was supposed to be a programme that was very balanced in terms of funding and tasks to be carried out. But Annex 2, a reserve list of PLN 40.5 billion, has also been introduced. It's not in the current version of the program, but that's what I'm going to talk about. After the consultation - the deadline for their completion was January 2015 - preparations for the extension of the programme began. As a result of these changes, a new programme structure with only one annex was created. And this list, which we are talking about today, which I have shown you and which is also available to you, is this list, which has been extended at the last minute and today is worth nearly PLN 200 billion in funding at the level of PLN 107 billion. ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-16-sejm-05-2.u476	Thank you very much. Dear Marshal, Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, In my parliamentary statement, I would like to introduce ladies and gentlemen with communication problems on the national road 52 from Bielsko-Biała to the popularly called Zakopane. This road is a key transport route linking Małopolska to Podbeskidzie and runs through the strict city centers of Andrychov and Kalwaria and crowded Wadowice and Kęta. Due to the close distances between cities and their linear construction and the increasing traffic and accident rate on the initiative of local authorities, the concept of building one common ring road called the Beskidzka Integration Road was created. It was originally to start its course in the vicinity of Kalwaria Zebrzydowska and end in the vicinity of Kęt and vice versa, extending its range between them neighbouring Wadowice and Andrychów. A systematic increase in traffic, followed by an increase in the number of accidents, led to the start of the first study work already in 1999. However, it was only the government of Prime Minister Jarosław Kaczyński, in which the Minister of Transport Jerzy Polaczek made a bold and expected decision to build the Beskidzki Integration Road, securing funds in the budget for the first preliminary project work. The vision of the future investment was then extended, changing its original shape from one-day to two-day road, as well as its range from Bielsko-Biała to Głogoczów, extending the previous assumptions closing only in the areas of Kalwaria Zebrzydowska, Wadowice, Andrychow and Kęt. This revived the hopes and expectations of local authorities to attract new investors, develop entrepreneurship, tourism and thus improve the existence of residents of the entire region. I am sorry to inform you that once the PO-PSL coalition took over the government in 2007 there was a halt to the preparatory work and a freeze to the prospects for the implementation of this investment, no place was found for it in national road and motorway construction programmes for individual years. In order to ensure the region's economic growth by maintaining and acquiring new investors, increasing the level of safety of its users, as well as the tourist development of the region, it is necessary to carry out this task quickly. That is why I have dared to familiarize you and your fellow Members with the motives and arguments for the need to implement this investment and to encourage action to build the Beskidska Integrative Way. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-16-sejm-05-2.u478	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to make a statement on the need to build a second water level in Wisla in the Kujawsko-Pomorskie voivodship. The need to build a second water level on the Vistula River has been mentioned for a long time. The existing dam, which is the only one regulating the state of the river, has been in operation for 45 years, while it was only supposed to last for 10 years. Although efforts have been made in recent years to implement this investment, it has still not been started and it is not known when it will occur. Another year goes by, and the threat of disaster continues to increase. Włocławska dam operates alone for almost half a century under conditions to which it was not designed. The investment on which human life may depend is constantly postponed and delayed. The consequences of this negligence can be tragic. The new water level is primarily the protection of residents and infrastructure against flood risks. The construction of the new dam is also to stop the erosion of the bottom river below Włocławka and further degradation of the trough of the lower Vistula, as well as to obtain an additional retention tank and increase groundwater, which would improve plant vegetation conditions. The creation of a second water barrier would also bring economic benefits, thanks to the possibility of obtaining renewable electricity, as well as new jobs and recovery in the region. The second dam would also be an additional crossing through the Vistula between Torun and Włocławek. In addition, a new flood on the river would be created, which would foster the development of tourism. I am very happy and I believe that the creation of the Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Shipping, as well as the Ministry of Energy, will speed up the implementation of this necessary and so very expected investment. In addition, the return to the environmental ministry of Professor Jan Szyszka and Mariusz Gajda, the people with competence and authority in the field of environmental protection, who understand how man can coexist with the environment and, consequently, see the possibility of making investments in space safely, is a guarantee of achieving without unnecessary delays this expected and needed investment for our country. The new firewall is estimated to cost about PLN 2 and 3 billion, which is about as much as the National Stadium, but the huge benefits of the dam are incomparable. It will be a source of clean, cheap and green electricity, it will create transport and sailing conditions in the upper section of the Vistula River, and most importantly - this investment will not generate costs, on the contrary - profits. After 6 years, according to the business plan, construction costs are to be reimbursed. It is high time to take decisive action to minimise the risk of a multi-dimensional disaster. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-16-sejm-05-2.u482	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Red is a village located in the Podlasie Voivodeship, in the Kolnen district, in the municipality of Kolno. This year, this mazowiecka village celebrated 600 years of existence. Let me tell you today about this town, ladies and gentlemen, because I believe that the actions taken by its inhabitants prove that even in such difficult times, times of crisis of values, in certain communities, tradition, history, homeland, patriotism is still important, alive and rediscovered, and what is happening in the commune of Kolno can be an example for others. The beginnings of the village Red dates back to the 15th century. The oldest document concerning Red dates back to 1415 and is a contract with Mayor Marcin Kostro for the location of Redstok on the law of Kolensk. In the document contained in the Main Archive of the Old Acts in Warsaw we read, and here, Mr. Marshal, I will join the text of the source attached. Translating and making available to the general public of this act was one of the important elements of the celebration of the 600th anniversary. The inhabitants of this village read the act during the celebrations, whose permanent souvenir is a boulder with a plaque and a inscription "In homage to all generations of inhabitants of the village Red in the 600th anniversary of existence" located in the center of Red, right next to the library and the house. Red and surroundings are very interesting in historical terms. Testimonies of the medieval history of this area are so-called. Baker's Mountain and Death Mountain by the Kozioł-Vincent road. These castles, along with others, defended the northern Mazowsze against the Prusai and Jaćwinga carriages. Another historically important object is the cross dedicated to January insurgents. It stands on the national road no. 63, just before entering the Red from the Kolna side. The cross was renewed in May 2013, the year of the 150th anniversary of the January Uprising. Next to the Municipal Public Library in Red stands a monument dedicated to Marshal Józef Piłsudski and fallen in the field of glory in 1918 and 1920. In this village there is a commune of the Public Library, the Primary School. Marshal Józef Piłsudski, there are also social organizations - voluntary firefighters, senior club, rural hostess, KaFcuki Association and the Redeemer's team. The initiatives of the inhabitants of this village, local organizations and institutions could be read not only in local media, but also on the websites of the Chancellery of the President of the Republic, the Senate of the Republic of Poland, and even heard from the speaker. This small town was presented in pictures before the Presidential Palace during the exhibition entitled: But don't step on the altars of the past, although you have to raise the altars more perfectly. Undoubtedly, it was a great honour to take the patronage of the President of the Republic of Poland in this year's celebration of the patronage of the primary school and the 5th anniversary of the team of Czerwieńcy within 25 years of self-government in Poland. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Today I mention this unique village to share with you the joy and pride. Here is a village with only a thousand inhabitants able to act, care for historical and cultural heritage, to surprise and embarrass larger, richer, better located towns. As a Member of the Parliament of the Republic, as well as honourable colleagues, I try to be in the field as often as possible, among people. In Red I felt that after 25 years of self-government there are places on the map of Poland, where regained freedom does not mean trampling on our faith and tradition, where modernity is not lacking rules, where it is possible to integrate different age groups for the sake of joint action, where traditions are still passed on from generation to generation, but also where residents learn new ones, e.g. the use of EU funds to meet local needs. There are places. Red is a place where you can really see the positive effects of freedom, solidarity, cooperation and activity, which draws from tradition and takes place with respect for faith and history. It's worth talking about out loud. In the last sentence of my speech, I would like to congratulate the people of the village Red Jubilee 600 years of existence and to thank you for the memory and living lesson of history, local patriotism and grassroots civic activity. Thank you. [[Applause]]
